> AGENT

Good — this confirms the auth fix is even more important than it first looked. Here's the prioritized list.

## The reliability finding that ties it together

Tokens are cached in **`storage.session`** (`auth.ts:20`, `KEY`), which Chrome **wipes on every browser restart**. So after each restart the nudge depends entirely on a silent re-mint — `getValidTokens()` → `mint(false)` with `prompt=none` (`auth.ts:96`). When that silent renew fails (no live Google session, third-party-cookie quirks, consent revoked), `doSync` hits its bare `catch { return }` (`background.ts:74-78`) and the **entire nudge mechanism goes dark with no badge, no banner, no notification, no error**. That's the single worst failure mode because it's both common (every restart is a roll of the dice) and invisible.

## Fixes, in priority order

**P0 — Surface auth-dead instead of dying silently.** *(background.ts `doSync`)*
Today `getValidTokens()` failure is indistinguishable from "never signed in." Split them: if `authStatus()` shows the user *was* signed in but the silent re-mint failed, set a distinct badge (e.g. `!` in a muted color) and push a "Reconnect Auxilio" banner state to calendar tabs. The user must be able to tell "nudging is offline" from "no visitor events right now." Low effort, no engine change, removes the scariest failure.

**P0 — Nudge on outstanding work, not magic-address presence.** *(calendar-sync.ts + the SEND path)*
Right now the badge/banner fire for any upcoming event with the magic address, including ones you already sent passes for — until 1h after the event ends, and dismissal only lives in `storage.session` so it resets on restart. Cheapest correct fix that respects the cost constraint: when the user sends passes through the panel (`SEND` in `background.ts`), record that event's `eventId`/`iCalUID` in a `handled` set in **`storage.local`** (durable), and filter it out of `listMarked()` / the banner / the badge count. Zero recurring cost, no engine polling, survives restarts.
- *Caveat to call out:* this only knows about passes sent *from this extension*. Passes sent via the add-on or another device won't suppress the nudge. True cross-channel correctness needs a lightweight engine "which of these iCalUIDs still need passes" endpoint — but that's an engine-contract change (hard rule #2), so I'd defer it and ship the local `handled` set first.

**P1 — Make dismissal durable.** *(calendar.content.ts `NUDGE_DISMISS_KEY`)*
It's in `storage.session` today, so dismissed events re-nag after a browser restart. Move to `storage.local`. Trivial, and it compounds the P0 noise fix.

**P1 — Tame recurring-event over-nudging.** *(calendar-sync.ts `runSync`)*
`singleEvents=true` means each instance is its own marked entry and each can fire its own OS notification via `newMarked` (`background.ts:94`). A weekly visitor standup becomes weekly notification spam. De-dupe notifications by the recurring series id (base of `<base>_<ts>`) so a series notifies once, not per instance.

**P2 — Primary-calendar blindness.** *(calendar.ts `listEvents`)*
Hardcoded `/calendars/primary/events`, so visitor meetings on a secondary calendar get no durable nudge. This one has real cost/complexity (multiple sync tokens, more API calls every minute) and a fuzzier payoff — I'd leave it documented as a known limitation unless someone actually books visitors on secondary calendars. Don't fix preemptively.

**P2 — Optimistic DOM scrape is best-effort.** Leave as-is. When Google collapses the guest list ("+3 more") the magic address can be missing from `innerText` and the instant banner misses — but the 1-min sync backstops it. Not worth hardening.

## What I'd actually ship first

The two P0s. They're both low-effort, both client-side (no engine change, no recurring cost — clean for the Ameya conversation), and together they fix the two things that make it look *broken* (silent death) and *dumb* (nagging about done work). The P1s are quick follow-ons in the same files.

Want me to implement the two P0s? They touch `background.ts`, `calendar-sync.ts`, and `auth.ts`/`messaging.ts` for the new badge/banner state — I can do them as one focused change.

1	/**
2	 * Typed RPC over chrome.runtime messaging. The side panel (and content script)
3	 * never touch tokens or the network directly — they send these messages and the
4	 * background worker does the work. This keeps the thin-adapter boundary: one
5	 * place holds secrets + network.
6	 */
7	import type {
8	  ActiveEvent,
9	  AuthStatus,
10	  DomEventSnapshot,
11	  DraftPatch,
12	  DraftResponse,
13	  PreviewResponse,
14	  SendResponse,
15	  VisitDraft,
16	  VisitorEventSummary,
17	} from './types';
18	
19	export type RpcRequest =
20	  | { type: 'AUTH_STATUS' }
21	  | { type: 'AUTH_SIGN_IN' }
22	  | { type: 'AUTH_SIGN_OUT' }
23	  | { type: 'OPEN_FOR_EVENT'; eid: string; snapshot?: DomEventSnapshot }
24	  | { type: 'FOLLOW_EVENT'; eid: string; snapshot?: DomEventSnapshot }
25	  | { type: 'RESOLVE_EVENT'; eid: string }
26	  | { type: 'DRAFT_LOAD'; event: ActiveEvent }
27	  | { type: 'DRAFT_PATCH'; iCalUid: string; patch: DraftPatch }
28	  | { type: 'PREVIEW'; iCalUid: string; visitorEmail: string }
29	  | { type: 'SEND'; iCalUid: string; start?: string; end?: string }
30	  | { type: 'CANCEL_GUEST'; iCalUid: string; invitationId: string }
31	  | { type: 'LIST_VISITOR_EVENTS' }
32	  | { type: 'GET_NUDGE_TARGETS' }
33	  | { type: 'SYNC_NOW' }
34	  | { type: 'GET_PANEL_STATE' }
35	  | { type: 'NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT'; eventId?: string; eid?: string };
36	
37	/** Maps each request type to its success payload. */
38	export interface RpcResultMap {
39	  AUTH_STATUS: AuthStatus;
40	  AUTH_SIGN_IN: AuthStatus;
41	  AUTH_SIGN_OUT: { signedIn: false };
42	  OPEN_FOR_EVENT: { opened: boolean };
43	  FOLLOW_EVENT: { followed: boolean };
44	  RESOLVE_EVENT: ActiveEvent;
45	  DRAFT_LOAD: DraftResponse;
46	  DRAFT_PATCH: VisitDraft;
47	  PREVIEW: PreviewResponse;
48	  SEND: SendResponse;
49	  CANCEL_GUEST: { invitationId: string; cancelled: boolean };
50	  LIST_VISITOR_EVENTS: VisitorEventSummary[];
51	  GET_NUDGE_TARGETS: VisitorEventSummary[];
52	  SYNC_NOW: { synced: boolean };
53	  GET_PANEL_STATE: { open: boolean };
54	  NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT: { navigated: boolean };
55	}
56	
57	export type RpcResponse<T> =
58	  | { ok: true; data: T }
59	  | { ok: false; error: string; status?: number; needsAuth?: boolean };
60	
61	export class RpcError extends Error {
62	  constructor(
63	    message: string,
64	    readonly status?: number,
65	    readonly needsAuth?: boolean,
66	  ) {
67	    super(message);
68	    this.name = 'RpcError';
69	  }
70	}
71	
72	/** Side-panel-side caller: throws RpcError on failure, returns typed data. */
73	export async function rpc<K extends RpcRequest['type']>(
74	  msg: Extract<RpcRequest, { type: K }>,
75	): Promise<RpcResultMap[K]> {
76	  const res = (await chrome.runtime.sendMessage(msg)) as
77	    | RpcResponse<RpcResultMap[K]>
78	    | undefined;
79	  if (!res) throw new RpcError('No response from background worker');
80	  if (!res.ok) throw new RpcError(res.error, res.status, res.needsAuth);
81	  return res.data;
82	}
83	
84	/** storage.session key holding the pending event id (set by background on the
85	 *  content-script button click, read reactively by the side panel). */
86	export const ACTIVE_EID_KEY = 'auxilio.activeEid';
87	
88	/** storage.session key holding the DOM snapshot for the pending event — lets the
89	 *  panel paint instantly + handle the unsaved (no-API) case. */
90	export const ACTIVE_SNAPSHOT_KEY = 'auxilio.activeSnapshot';
91	
92	/** Background→content broadcast of side-panel open state, so the in-page button
93	 *  hides while the panel is open (no redundant control). */
94	export const PANEL_STATE = '__auxilio_panel_state';
95	
96	/** Port name the side panel connects on mount so background can track open/close. */
97	export const SIDEPANEL_PORT = 'auxilio.sidepanel';
98	
99	/** Background→content broadcast of the visitor events needing passes (drives the
100	 *  in-page nudge banner). */
101	export const NUDGE_TARGETS = '__auxilio_nudge_targets';
102	
103	/** One-way background→panel broadcast: the active event changed on the server
104	 *  (post-save), so the panel should refetch. Not an RPC (no response). */
105	export const REFRESH_ACTIVE = '__auxilio_refresh_active';
106	
107	export function broadcastRefreshActive(): void {
108	  chrome.runtime.sendMessage({ type: REFRESH_ACTIVE }).catch(() => {});
109	}
110	
```

1	import { LogIn } from 'lucide-react';
2	import { Button, Card } from '@/design/components';
3	import { useSignIn } from './hooks';
4	
5	/** Shown when the engine/Calendar call needs a signed-in host. */
6	export function SignInGate({ reason }: { reason?: string }) {
7	  const signIn = useSignIn();
8	  return (
9	    <div style={{ padding: 'var(--space-lg)' }}>
10	      <Card
11	        style={{
12	          display: 'flex',
13	          flexDirection: 'column',
14	          alignItems: 'center',
15	          textAlign: 'center',
16	          gap: 'var(--space-md)',
17	          padding: 'var(--space-xl)',
18	        }}
19	      >
20	        <span className="type-title-lg">Sign in to continue</span>
21	        <span className="type-body text-muted">
22	          {reason ??
23	            'Connect your Google account to load guests and send visitor passes.'}
24	        </span>
25	        <Button
26	          icon={<LogIn size={18} strokeWidth={2} />}
27	          loading={signIn.isPending}
28	          onClick={() => signIn.mutate()}
29	        >
30	          Sign in with Google
31	        </Button>
32	        {signIn.isError && (
33	          <span className="type-label-sm" style={{ color: 'var(--color-error)' }}>
34	            {(signIn.error as Error).message}
35	          </span>
36	        )}
37	      </Card>
38	    </div>
39	  );
40	}
41

1	import { useEffect, useState } from 'react';
2	import { useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
3	import {
4	  AlertCircle,
5	  ArrowLeft,
6	  CheckCircle2,
7	  Clock,
8	  DoorOpen,
9	  ExternalLink,
10	  Eye,
11	  RefreshCw,
12	} from 'lucide-react';
13	import {
14	  Button,
15	  Card,
16	  IconButton,
17	  Logo,
18	  SelectField,
19	  Skeleton,
20	} from '@/design/components';
21	import {
22	  ACTIVE_EID_KEY,
23	  ACTIVE_SNAPSHOT_KEY,
24	  REFRESH_ACTIVE,
25	  RpcError,
26	  rpc,
27	} from '@/lib/messaging';
28	import type { PreviewResponse } from '@/lib/types';
29	import {
30	  useActiveEid,
31	  useActiveSnapshot,
32	  useAuthStatus,
33	  useDraft,
34	  usePatchDraft,
35	  usePreview,
36	  useResolveEvent,
37	  useSend,
38	  useVisitorEvents,
39	} from './hooks';
40	import { AccountMenu } from './AccountMenu';
41	import { EmptyState } from './EmptyState';
42	import { RosterRow } from './RosterRow';
43	import { PreviewSheet } from './PreviewSheet';
44	import { SignInGate } from './SignInGate';
45	
46	const isAuthError = (err: unknown) => err instanceof RpcError && !!err.needsAuth;
47	
48	export function VisitPanel() {
49	  // `storedEid` is what the content script is pointing us at (auto-follow). We
50	  // freeze the *displayed* `eid` while busy so following never switches the view
51	  // mid-action (Guard 1); edits already persist on blur (Guard 2).
52	  const storedEid = useActiveEid();
53	  const snapshot = useActiveSnapshot();
54	  const [eid, setEid] = useState<string | null>(storedEid);
55	  const auth = useAuthStatus();
56	  // Picker fuels the "open from list" empty state (and graceful fallback).
57	  const visitorEvents = useVisitorEvents(!eid && !!auth.data?.signedIn);
58	  const resolve = useResolveEvent(eid);
59	  const event = resolve.data;
60	  const draft = useDraft(event);
61	
62	  const patch = usePatchDraft(event?.iCalUid);
63	  const send = useSend(event?.iCalUid, event?.start, event?.end);
64	  const preview = usePreview(event?.iCalUid);
65	
66	  const [previewData, setPreviewData] = useState<{
67	    preview: PreviewResponse;
68	    recipient: string;
69	  } | null>(null);
70	  // Tracks host edits the roster can't otherwise reveal — a name/phone change or
71	  // template switch on an already-issued event leaves no structural delta, so we
72	  // remember "the host touched something" to keep the update button live. Reset
73	  // when the event changes or a send succeeds (the draft then reflects reality).
74	  const [edited, setEdited] = useState(false);
75	  useEffect(() => setEdited(false), [event?.iCalUid]);
76	
77	  // Guard 1: hold the current event while sending / previewing; catch up once idle.
78	  const busy = send.isPending || preview.isPending || !!previewData;
79	  useEffect(() => {
80	    if (!busy) setEid(storedEid);
81	  }, [storedEid, busy]);
82	
83	  // Post-save refresh: the background sync poll broadcasts when the active event
84	  // changed on the server (no DOM); refetch so the roster reflects saved state.
85	  const qc = useQueryClient();
86	  useEffect(() => {
87	    const listener = (msg: { type?: string }) => {
88	      if (msg?.type === REFRESH_ACTIVE) {
89	        qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['resolve'] });
90	        qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['draft'] });
91	      }
92	    };
93	    chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener(listener);
94	    return () => chrome.runtime.onMessage.removeListener(listener);
95	  }, [qc]);
96	
97	  // Back → clear the active event/snapshot, returning to the home list.
98	  const clearActive = () =>
99	    chrome.storage.session.remove([ACTIVE_EID_KEY, ACTIVE_SNAPSHOT_KEY]);
100	  // Show Back in every screen except the pure home (no event, no snapshot).
101	  const back = eid || snapshot?.magicPresent ? clearActive : undefined;
102	
103	  // ---- gating states ----
104	  if (!eid) {
105	    // New, not-yet-saved visitor event (no eid, but the DOM saw the magic
106	    // address) → preview the detected guests + prompt to save.
107	    if (snapshot?.magicPresent) {
108	      return (
109	        <Shell onBack={back}>
110	          <NoticeState
111	            title="Save the event to issue passes"
112	            body="This event isn’t saved yet, so passes can’t be issued. Add your guests and save it in Google Calendar, then reopen Manage Visitors."
113	            guests={snapshot.guestEmails}
114	          />
115	        </Shell>
116	      );
117	    }
118	    return (
119	      <Shell>
120	        <EmptyState
121	          auth={auth.data}
122	          events={visitorEvents.data}
123	          loading={visitorEvents.isLoading}
124	          onPick={(ev) => {
125	            // Just point the panel at the picked event — no tab navigation. Use
126	            // "Open in Calendar" in the summary to jump to it explicitly.
127	            chrome.storage.session.set({ [ACTIVE_EID_KEY]: ev.eid });
128	          }}
129	        />
130	      </Shell>
131	    );
132	  }
133	  if (isAuthError(resolve.error) || isAuthError(draft.error)) {
134	    return <Shell onBack={back}><SignInGate /></Shell>;
135	  }
136	  if (resolve.isLoading) return <Shell onBack={back}><LoadingState label="Reading event…" /></Shell>;
137	  if (resolve.isError) {
138	    const msg = (resolve.error as Error).message;
139	    if (msg === 'NOT_SAVED') {
140	      return (
141	        <Shell onBack={back}>
142	          <NoticeState
143	            title="Save the event to issue passes"
144	            body="This event isn’t saved yet, so passes can’t be issued. Save it in Google Calendar, then reopen Manage Visitors."
145	            onRetry={() => resolve.refetch()}
146	            guests={snapshot?.guestEmails}
147	          />
148	        </Shell>
149	      );
150	    }
151	    return (
152	      <Shell onBack={back}>
153	        <ErrorState message={msg} onRetry={() => resolve.refetch()} />
154	      </Shell>
155	    );
156	  }
157	  // Event resolved but no iCalUID → the draft query can't run (would sit on a
158	  // skeleton forever). Surface it instead of hanging.
159	  if (event && !event.iCalUid) {
160	    return (
161	      <Shell onBack={back}>
162	        <NoticeState
163	          title="Can’t register from this event"
164	          body="This event has no calendar UID yet. This usually means it is not fully saved. Save it in Google Calendar and try again."
165	          onRetry={() => resolve.refetch()}
166	        />
167	      </Shell>
168	    );
169	  }
170	  if (draft.isError) {
171	    return (
172	      <Shell onBack={back}>
173	        <ErrorState message={(draft.error as Error).message} onRetry={() => draft.refetch()} />
174	      </Shell>
175	    );
176	  }
177	  if (!draft.data) {
178	    return <Shell onBack={back}><RosterSkeleton title={event?.title} /></Shell>;
179	  }
180	
181	  const data = draft.data;
182	  const included = data.roster.filter((g) => g.include);
183	  const sentCount = data.roster.filter((g) => g.status === 'sent').length;
184	
185	  // What's actually pending. A new/uninvited guest still to get a pass, or a sent
186	  // guest toggled off (a pending revoke), is a structural change visible in the
187	  // roster. Name/phone/template edits aren't — `edited` covers those.
188	  const pendingNew = data.roster.filter(
189	    (g) => g.include && g.status !== 'sent' && g.status !== 'cancelled',
190	  );
191	  const pendingCancel = data.roster.filter((g) => g.status === 'sent' && !g.include);
192	  const hasChanges = pendingNew.length > 0 || pendingCancel.length > 0 || edited;
193	  // Already-issued event with literally nothing to do → no point re-sending.
194	  const nothingToDo = data.materialized && !hasChanges;
195	
196	  const templates = data.emailTemplates;
197	  // One template for the whole event (engine stores per-guest; we set all guests
198	  // to the chosen key).
199	  const currentTemplate =
200	    data.roster.find((g) => g.emailTemplateKey)?.emailTemplateKey ??
201	    templates.find((t) => t.isDefault)?.key ??
202	    templates[0]?.key;
203	
204	  const patchGuest = (email: string, edit: Record<string, unknown>) => {
205	    setEdited(true);
206	    patch.mutate({ guests: [{ email, ...edit }] });
207	  };
208	
209	  const setEventTemplate = (key: string) => {
210	    setEdited(true);
211	    patch.mutate({ guests: data.roster.map((g) => ({ email: g.email, emailTemplateKey: key })) });
212	  };
213	
214	  // Review & send: render the real email for a representative included guest,
215	  // then Send/Cancel from the preview.
216	  const reviewEmail = included[0]?.email;
217	  const openReview = () => {
218	    if (!reviewEmail) return;
219	    preview.mutate(reviewEmail, {
220	      onSuccess: (d) => setPreviewData({ preview: d, recipient: reviewEmail }),
221	    });
222	  };
223	
224	  // First send needs at least one included guest; an already-issued event needs a
225	  // real pending change — otherwise the button is irrelevant and stays disabled.
226	  const canSend =
227	    !send.isPending && (data.materialized ? hasChanges : included.length > 0);
228	  const reviewLabel = data.materialized ? 'Review & update passes' : 'Review & send passes';
229	
230	  return (
231	    <Shell onBack={back}>
232	      <div className="enter" style={{ padding: 'var(--space-lg)', display: 'grid', gap: 'var(--space-lg)' }}>
233	        {/* Event summary */}
234	        <Card style={{ display: 'grid', gap: 'var(--space-sm)' }}>
235	          <div className="type-title-lg">{event?.title || 'Untitled event'}</div>
236	          {event?.start && (
237	            <div className="type-body text-muted" style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 6 }}>
238	              <Clock size={15} strokeWidth={2} />
239	              {formatWhen(event.start, event.end)}
240	            </div>
241	          )}
242	          {event?.rooms && event.rooms.length > 0 && (
243	            <div className="type-body text-muted" style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 6 }}>
244	              <DoorOpen size={15} strokeWidth={2} />
245	              <span className="row__ellipsis">{event.rooms.join(', ')}</span>
246	            </div>
247	          )}
248	          {event?.description && (
249	            <div
250	              className="type-label-sm text-muted"
251	              style={{
252	                whiteSpace: 'pre-wrap',
253	                maxHeight: 72,
254	                overflow: 'hidden',
255	                borderTop: '1px solid var(--color-outline-variant)',
256	                paddingTop: 'var(--space-sm)',
257	              }}
258	            >
259	              {event.description}
260	            </div>
261	          )}
262	          {eid && (
263	            <Button
264	              variant="text"
265	              icon={<ExternalLink size={16} strokeWidth={2} />}
266	              onClick={() => void rpc({ type: 'NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT', eid })}
267	              // Negative left margin = the text button's own horizontal padding,
268	              // so the label optically aligns to the card edge while the hit area
269	              // and hover state layer stay intact (MD3 text-button placement).
270	              style={{ justifySelf: 'start', marginLeft: 'calc(-1 * var(--space-md))' }}
271	            >
272	              Open in Calendar
273	            </Button>
274	          )}
275	          {templates.length >= 2 && (
276	            <SelectField
277	              label="Email template (all guests)"
278	              value={currentTemplate}
279	              onChange={(e) => setEventTemplate(e.target.value)}
280	            >
281	              {templates.map((t) => (
282	                <option key={t.key} value={t.key}>
283	                  {t.name}
284	                  {t.isDefault ? ' (default)' : ''}
285	                </option>
286	              ))}
287	            </SelectField>
288	          )}
289	        </Card>
290	
291	        {/* Roster */}
292	        <section>
293	          <div
294	            className="type-label-sm text-muted"
295	            style={{ textTransform: 'uppercase', marginBottom: 4 }}
296	          >
297	            Guests · {included.length} getting a pass
298	          </div>
299	          {data.roster.length === 0 ? (
300	            <Card className="type-body text-muted">
301	              No guests on this event yet. Add attendees in Calendar, then reopen.
302	            </Card>
303	          ) : (
304	            <div className="roster">
305	              {data.roster.map((g) => (
306	                <RosterRow
307	                  key={g.email}
308	                  guest={g}
309	                  onChange={(edit) => patchGuest(g.email, edit)}
310	                />
311	              ))}
312	            </div>
313	          )}
314	        </section>
315	
316	        {/* Send result */}
317	        {send.isSuccess && <SendSummary result={send.data} />}
318	        {send.isError && (

321	            <span className="type-body">{(send.error as Error).message}</span>

332	        }}
333	      >
334	        {nothingToDo && (

342	          </div>

344	        <Button
345	          block

349	          onClick={openReview}

358	          </div>

360	        {sentCount > 0 && !send.isPending && !nothingToDo && (

362	            {sentCount} pass{sentCount > 1 ? 'es' : ''} already issued
363	          </div>
364	        )}
365	      </footer>
366	
367	      {previewData && (
368	        <PreviewSheet
369	          preview={previewData.preview}
370	          recipient={previewData.recipient}
371	          totalCount={included.length}
372	          sending={send.isPending}
373	          onSend={() =>

380	          }

382	        />
383	      )}
384	    </Shell>
385	  );
386	}

534	            : result.cancelled.length > 0

538	        </div>

544	              </li>

552	
553	function formatWhen(start: string, end?: string): string {
554	  const allDay = !start.includes('T');
555	  const s = new Date(start);
556	  const date = s.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { weekday: 'short', day: 'numeric', month: 'short' });
557	  if (allDay) return `${date} · All day`;
558	  const t = (d: Date) => d.toLocaleTimeString(undefined, { hour: 'numeric', minute: '2-digit' });
559	  return end ? `${date} · ${t(s)} to ${t(new Date(end))}` : `${date} · ${t(s)}`;
560	}

  "new_string": "const KEY = 'auxilio.tokens';\n\n/** Durable (storage.local) marker that the user has connected and intends to stay\n *  connected. Token bundles live in storage.session and are WIPED on every browser\n *  restart, so an absent bundle can't tell \"never signed in\" from \"session cleared,\n *  silent renew due.\" This flag survives restarts: while it's set, a failed silent\n *  renew means auth has lapsed (surface a reconnect), not that the user is signed\n *  out. Cleared only on explicit sign-out. */\nconst CONNECTED_KEY = 'auxilio.connected';"
}

  "old_string": "export async function signOut(): Promise<void> {\n  const cached = await readCache();\n  if (cached?.accessToken) {\n    await fetch(\n      `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke?token=${cached.accessToken}`,\n      { method: 'POST' },\n    ).catch(() => {});\n  }\n  await chrome.storage.session.remove(KEY);\n}\n\nexport async function authStatus(): Promise<AuthStatus> {\n  const cached = await readCache();\n  if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) {\n    return { signedIn: true, email: cached.email };\n  }\n  return { signedIn: false };\n}",
  "new_string": "export async function signOut(): Promise<void> {\n  const cached = await readCache();\n  if (cached?.accessToken) {\n    await fetch(\n      `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke?token=${cached.accessToken}`,\n      { method: 'POST' },\n    ).catch(() => {});\n  }\n  await chrome.storage.session.remove(KEY);\n  await chrome.storage.local.remove(CONNECTED_KEY);\n}\n\nexport async function authStatus(): Promise<AuthStatus> {\n  const cached = await readCache();\n  if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) {\n    return { signedIn: true, email: cached.email };\n  }\n  return { signedIn: false };\n}\n\n/** Did the user connect at least once and not explicitly sign out? Survives the\n *  restart that wipes the storage.session token cache — so the background can tell\n *  a recoverable auth lapse from a genuine signed-out state. */\nexport async function wasConnected(): Promise<boolean> {\n  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(CONNECTED_KEY);\n  return !!r[CONNECTED_KEY];\n}"

  "new_string": "const SYNC_TOKEN_KEY = 'auxilio.syncToken';\nconst MARKED_KEY = 'auxilio.markedEvents';\n/** Events the user has already issued passes for from this extension — keyed by\n *  eventId, value = when handled. A durable overlay over the marked set so a\n *  finished event stops badging/notifying/nudging instead of nagging until it\n *  ages out. Local-only signal: passes sent via the add-on or another device\n *  won't appear here (full cross-channel truth needs an engine status endpoint —\n *  deferred). Pruned by HANDLED_TTL_MS. */\nconst HANDLED_KEY = 'auxilio.handledEvents';\nconst PAST_GRACE_MS = 60 * 60_000; // keep events until 1h after they end\nconst HANDLED_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60_000; // forget \"handled\" after 30 days\n\ntype MarkedMap = Record<string, VisitorEventSummary>;\ntype HandledMap = Record<string, number>;\n\nasync function readMarked(): Promise<MarkedMap> {\n  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(MARKED_KEY);\n  return (r[MARKED_KEY] as MarkedMap) ?? {};\n}\n\nasync function readHandled(): Promise<HandledMap> {\n  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(HANDLED_KEY);\n  return (r[HANDLED_KEY] as HandledMap) ?? {};\n}\n\n/** Record that passes were issued for this event — drops it from every nudge\n *  surface (badge, banner, notification, picker) until it ages out. */\nexport async function markHandled(eventId: string): Promise<void> {\n  if (!eventId) return;\n  const handled = await readHandled();\n  handled[eventId] = Date.now();\n  await chrome.storage.local.set({ [HANDLED_KEY]: handled });\n}"
}

  "new_string": "async function doSync(): Promise<void> {\n  let accessToken: string;\n  try {\n    accessToken = (await getValidTokens()).accessToken;\n  } catch {\n    // Token unavailable. If the user was connected, this is a recoverable lapse\n    // (storage.session wiped on restart, or silent renew failed) — surface it so\n    // nudging never dies silently. If they never connected, stay quiet.\n    if (await wasConnected()) await setLapsed(true);\n    return;\n  }\n  let result;\n  try {\n    result = await runSync(accessToken);\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.warn('[auxilio] sync failed', err);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // Got here with working tokens → any prior lapse is over.\n  await setLapsed(false);\n\n  // Toolbar badge = number of upcoming visitor events (the on-icon nudge).\n  await chrome.action.setBadgeBackgroundColor({ color: BADGE_COLOR });\n  await chrome.action.setBadgeText({\n    text: result.markedCount > 0 ? String(result.markedCount) : '',\n  });"

  "new_string": "function broadcastNudge(targets: VisitorEventSummary[], cancelled: string[] = []): void {\n  sendToCalendarTabs({ type: NUDGE_TARGETS, targets, cancelled });\n}\n\n/** Enter/leave the recoverable auth-lapsed state: a distinct toolbar badge plus a\n *  broadcast that drives the in-page \"Reconnect\" banner. Broadcast unconditionally\n *  so a calendar tab loaded mid-lapse learns about it on the next sync. */\nasync function setLapsed(lapsed: boolean): Promise<void> {\n  if (lapsed) {\n    await chrome.action.setBadgeBackgroundColor({ color: LAPSED_BADGE_COLOR });\n    await chrome.action.setBadgeText({ text: '!' });\n  }\n  sendToCalendarTabs({ type: AUTH_LAPSED, lapsed });\n}\n\n/** Recompute the nudge surfaces from local state (no Calendar round-trip) — used\n *  after a send marks an event handled, so the badge/banner drop it immediately. */\nasync function refreshNudgeSurfaces(): Promise<void> {\n  const targets = await listMarked();\n  await chrome.action.setBadgeBackgroundColor({ color: BADGE_COLOR });\n  await chrome.action.setBadgeText({ text: targets.length > 0 ? String(targets.length) : '' });\n  broadcastNudge(targets);\n}"
}

'position:fixed;top:120px;left:50%;transform:translateX(-50%);z-index:2147483647;pointer-events:none;';\n  document.documentElement.appendChild(host);\n  const root = host.attachShadow({ mode: 'open' });\n  root.innerHTML = `\n    <style>\n      :host { all: initial; }\n      .banner {\n        display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;max-width:520px;\n        font-family:'Google Sans','Roboto',system-ui,sans-serif;color:#410002;\n        background:#fcdad6;border:1px solid #b3261e33;border-radius:14px;\n        padding:10px 12px 10px 16px;box-shadow:0 4px 12px rgba(0,0,0,.18);\n        transform:translateY(-12px);opacity:0;pointer-events:none;\n        transition:opacity 200ms cubic-bezier(.2,0,0,1),transform 200ms cubic-bezier(.2,0,0,1);\n      }\n      .banner.show{opacity:1;transform:none;pointer-events:auto;}\n      .icon{display:inline-flex;color:#b3261e;flex:0 0 auto;}\n      .text{font-size:13.5px;line-height:18px;}\n      .text b{font-weight:600;}\n      .reconnect{font:inherit;font-size:13px;font-weight:600;color:#fff;background:#b3261e;\n        border:none;border-radius:999px;padding:8px 14px;cursor:pointer;flex:0 0 auto;}\n      .reconnect:hover{box-shadow:0 2px 6px rgba(0,0,0,.2);}\n      @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce){.banner{transition:none;transform:none;}}\n    </style>\n    <div class=\"reconnect\" type=\"button\">Reconnect</button>\n    </div>`;\n  const wrap = root.querySelector('.banner') as HTMLDivElement;\n  const btn = root.querySelector('.reconnect') as HTMLButtonElement;\n  shieldInteractions(host);\n\n  let lapsed = false;\n  let panelOpen = false;\n  function render() {\n    wrap.classList.toggle('show', lapsed && !panelOpen);\n  }\n\n  // > AGENT

Both P0s are implemented, type-checked, and built clean (v0.5.0).

## What changed

**P0a — Nudge on outstanding work, not magic-address presence**
- `lib/calendar-sync.ts`: added a durable `handledEvents` overlay in `storage.local` keyed by `eventId` (30-day TTL, pruned on each sync, and purged if the event is later cancelled/de-marked). `runSync` now excludes handled events from `newMarked` (no re-notify) and from the badge count; `listMarked()` filters them out so the banner and picker drop them too. New exported `markHandled()`.
- `entrypoints/background.ts`: the `SEND` handler now calls `markHandled(providerEventId)` when the send leaves the event with `activeCount > 0`, then `refreshNudgeSurfaces()` recomputes the badge + banner from local state immediately — so a handled event stops nagging the moment passes go out, and stays gone across browser restarts.
- *Documented limitation in the code:* this is a local signal — passes sent via the add-on or another device won't suppress the nudge. True cross-channel truth needs an engine status endpoint, which I deliberately left out (it'd be an engine-contract change, hard rule #2).

**P0b — Surface auth-dead instead of dying silently**
- `lib/auth.ts`: added a durable `connected` flag in `storage.local` (set on sign-in, cleared on sign-out) plus `wasConnected()`. This is what distinguishes "never signed in" from "session token cache wiped on restart / silent renew failed" — the `tokens` bundle lives in `storage.session`, which Chrome wipes every restart.
- `entrypoints/background.ts`: `doSync`'s previously-silent token-failure path now, if `wasConnected()`, enters a lapse state — a distinct red `!` toolbar badge and an `AUTH_LAPSED` broadcast. On the next successful sync it clears the lapse. Added an `OPEN_PANEL` RPC.
- `entrypoints/calendar.content.ts`: new in-page "Auxilio needs to reconnect — visitor nudges are paused" banner (`mountReconnect`), driven by `AUTH_LAPSED`, with a Reconnect button that opens the side panel onto the sign-in gate. Hidden while the panel is open.

## What I did not touch
Per the priority discussion, I left the P1s (durable dismissal, recurring de-dupe) and P2s (secondary calendars, DOM scrape hardening) alone — say the word and I'll knock out the two P1s in the same files.

'~').localeCompare(existing.start ?? '~') < 0) {\n      bySeries.set(key, m);\n    }\n  }\n  return [...bySeries.values()].sort((a, b) =>\n    (a.start ?? '').localeCompare(b.start ?? ''),\n  );\n}"
}

> AGENT

Working through these carefully, two of the four flipped on me. Here's where I landed, then one decision I need from you.

## P1b — recurring over-nudging → **done** (v0.5.1)
A recurring visitor series is now one nudge unit: one OS notification (de-duped by series within a sync *and* against already-tracked series), the badge counts distinct pending series (not 52 weekly instances), and the picker/banner show the **soonest pending occurrence** per series. Handling one occurrence (`markHandled` on its instance id) surfaces the next — which is correct, since each occurrence is a separate visit. Uses the API's authoritative `recurringEventId`, not id-string splitting.

## P1a — durable dismissal → **reversed, deliberately not doing it**
I recommended this, but P0a changed the calculus and now it'd be a *regression*. After P0a, durable suppression already happens exactly when work is actually done (`markHandled` on send). The `X` dismiss is now a pure **session snooze**. If I made it durable, one stray `X` on an event that still needs passes would hide it *forever* — which directly violates the accountability goal ("overdue items should resurface"). So session-scoped dismissal is the right design now: done work is suppressed durably, un-done work resurfaces next session. Leaving it.

## P2b — DOM scrape hardening → **recommend against**
The only failure is a collapsed guest list ("+3 more") where the magic address isn't in the visible text, so the *instant* optimistic banner misses — but the 1-min sync still catches it. Hardening means programmatically expanding Google's guest list, i.e. exactly the layout/markup coupling the content script's design explicitly forbids. Trading a robustness principle for ~1 min of latency on an edge case is a bad deal. Leave it.

## P2a — secondary-calendar blindness → **needs your call**
This is the one real gap left, but the fix isn't free. `listEvents` only polls `/calendars/primary`. To cover owned secondary calendars I have to add the **`calendar.calendarlist.readonly`** OAuth scope (to discover them via `calendarList.list`) and poll each owned calendar on its own sync token. Cost-wise the polling is modest (incremental sync tokens → near-empty calls; owners typically have 1–3 writable calendars). The real cost is the **scope addition → re-consent**, which is the Ameya-lens tradeoff for a gap that may not even occur in practice.

What would you like to clarify about P2a? Happy to dig into any of it — for example:

- **Whether the gap is even real for us** — do hosts at Appointy ever create visitor events on a secondary calendar they own (e.g. a shared "Front Desk" or team calendar), or does everything land on their primary? If it's always primary, P2a is moot.
- **The re-consent friction** — since we're internal-first + force-installed, a Workspace admin can usually pre-approve the new scope so users never see a consent prompt. So "re-consent" may be a non-issue for our deployment, only mattering if/when we go public.
- **The actual recurring cost** — concrete per-minute API call math for the multi-calendar poll, for the Ameya conversation.
- **Scope alternatives** — whether there's a way to cover secondary calendars without `calendar.calendarlist.readonly`.

Tell me what's on your mind and I'll reframe the decision accordingly.

> DEVELOPER

which one do you recommend?

> AGENT

Defer it — Option 1.

My reasoning, in order of weight:

1. **The gap is speculative.** I have zero evidence anyone at Appointy creates visitor events on a secondary calendar they own. Visitor events are almost always on the host's primary. Building multi-calendar polling now is solving a problem we haven't observed — which cuts against both the cost constraint and the "don't build ahead of need" principle.

2. **Deferring costs us nothing later.** The scope addition isn't cheaper to do now than in three months. Because we're internal-first and force-installed, whenever we *do* add `calendar.calendarlist.readonly`, a Workspace admin can pre-approve it so users never see a re-consent prompt. There's no "do it now to avoid friction later" argument — the friction is ~zero either way.

3. **It adds permanent surface area for a maybe.** More scope on the consent screen, more code paths, more per-minute API calls forever — all carried indefinitely to cover a case that may never happen. That's exactly the kind of quiet bloat the four constraints exist to prevent, and the wrong thing to walk into the Ameya conversation having added.

So: leave it primary-only, drop a code comment at `listEvents` and a one-line memory noting the limitation and the trigger to revisit (first real report of a visitor event missed because it was on a secondary calendar). **And** if that trigger ever fires, go straight to Option 3 (owner-only) — never poll subscribed/holiday calendars.

The one thing that would change my mind: if you already *know* of a shared team/front-desk calendar that hosts use for visitor bookings. If that's a real workflow today, it jumps to Option 3 now. Is it?

Want me to add the comment + memory note and call P1/P2 done?

9	
10	A Chrome extension is being considered as a **desktop** surface for calendar visitor registration — richer UX than the add-on (realtime guest updates, template preview, inline edits) but **desktop Chrome only** and carrying a permanent DOM-maintenance tax. It does **not** replace the add-on; the two are complementary, unified by the **magic address** that marks an event as a visitor event (one signal, many reactors: extension on desktop, add-on on mobile, engine watch for async FCM/WhatsApp/Chat nudges).
11	
12	Decisions locked (2026-06-18):
13	- **UX = Option A:** detect magic address → auto-inject dismissible in-page banner (no gesture) → one click opens the **native** Chrome side panel. (`sidePanel.open()` needs a user gesture; Option B = zero-click injected overlay, deferred.)
14	- **OAuth stays in the engine** (one app to verify); extension is a thin client (identity + DOM read + calls engine). Async = FCM/Web Push, never held sockets (MV3 SW can't hold them).
15	- **Internal-first** (force-install, unlisted, zero verification) to prove the flow, then public.
16	- **Public/commercial is approvable** IF: host perms = `calendar.google.com` only, no remote code (MV3), privacy policy + Limited Use, non-deceptive branding, **Calendar-only (no CASA)**. Can't charge via Web Store (bill via engine); going commercial = data-processor/DPA/GDPR layer to raise with leadership.
17	- **Validate first:** are hosts actually on desktop, and is the UX win worth the DOM tax.
18	
19	Build status (2026-06-18): repo **built** at `auxilio-extension/` (own git, sibling like auxilio-workspace). WXT 0.20 + React 19 + TS + TanStack Query + Zustand + lucide. **Phase 0 (scaffold)** + **Phase 1 (manual-invoke full flow)** done, committed, `tsc`+`wxt build` green. Phase 1 = content-script shadow-DOM "Register a visitor" button (URL eid detection, not DOM scrape) → background SW (sole token/network holder; launchWebAuthFlow id_token+access_token, Calendar events.get → canonical iCalUID, typed `/addon/*` client) → side panel (roster switches, blur-persisted name/phone edits, template select, sandboxed email preview, send). Reuses engine `/api/visitor/calendar/addon/{draft,preview,send,cancel-guest}` verbatim — **only engine change needed = add the extension's OAuth client id to `AUXILIO_WORKSPACE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`**. To RUN: create a Google **Web** OAuth client (redirect `https://<ext-id>.chromiumapp.org/`), set `WXT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` in `.env`, add same id to engine env allow-list.
20	
21	**Phase 2 finding — magic-address auto-ingest is RETIRED** (engine D5, 2026-06-17: `routes.ts:504`, `/calendar/inbound` returns 410 unless `VISITOR_CALENDAR_INBOUND_ENABLED=1`). The marker address still exists as env `VISITOR_CALENDAR_INBOX_ADDRESS` and is still stripped from attendees (`calendar-ingest.ts:81`). So the "one signal, many reactors" auto-ISSUE premise is weaker — but the address still works as the extension's **intentional client-side TRIGGER** (detect it among attendees → auto-banner), which matches the user's "intentional, not every event" vision. Phase 2 should key off `VISITOR_CALENDAR_INBOX_ADDRESS` (align `WXT_MAGIC_ADDRESS` to it), not auto-issuance. **Phase 2 BUILT** (committed, green): content script text-scans for the magic address → auto-injects the dismissible Option-A banner on marked events (unmarked events keep the subtle FAB; per-event dismissal falls back to FAB); debounced MutationObserver → `EVENT_TOUCHED` broadcast → panel refetches. Honest limitation: "live" = refetch-on-change, authoritative AFTER Calendar save (engine draft is API-sourced); true pre-save attendee mirroring would need DOM scraping — deferred. **Phase 3 BUILT** (committed, green): account menu + sign-out (clears caches → re-gates), WXT options page (account + read-only engine/magic-address config), toolbar **badge** on marked events (content→background setBadgeText — the on-icon nudge the add-on can't do), a11y (focus-visible rings, role=status/alert live regions). **Deliberately deferred & flagged (need user decision, not slipped in): event write-back '✓ Registered' (requires OAuth WRITE-scope escalation — currently calendar.events.readonly only) and editable engine URL (fixed host_permissions can't fetch arbitrary hosts).** Commits on `auxilio-extension` main: b12b725 (P0), d9be807 (P1), a6ee08d (P2), 0e8e22b (P3), f7930e7 (**Option B hardening**).
22	
23	**Architecture decision (2026-06-19): adopted Option B** (extension-primary, hardened) — may move to Option A (hybrid: add-on owns in-event, extension owns the rest) if B doesn't suffice. Option B as built: DOM reliance contained to the SINGLE `data-eventid`-on-click signal (→ FAB); change detection is the official sync-token poll — background `chrome.alarms` (~1min) → Calendar `events.list` w/ syncToken (`lib/calendar-sync.ts`) → maintains upcoming visitor-event set → `chrome.notifications` + toolbar badge + `REFRESH_ACTIVE` broadcast (post-save refetch). New `EmptyState.tsx` = sign-in (signed-out) / open-from-list picker (signed-in) = the graceful fallback if click-detection breaks. Removed: magic-address DOM scan, in-page banner, MutationObserver. **Consciously NOT done (add-on-only, not robust in any extension): pre-save live edit mirroring + auto-open** — reflects SAVED state only.
24	
25	**Nudge/Manage fixes (2026-06-19):** (1) BUG — `chrome.storage.session` is trusted-context-only by default, so content-script reads/writes silently failed (nudge dismissals + active-event handoff). Fixed: background calls `chrome.storage.session.setAccessLevel({accessLevel:'TRUSTED_AND_UNTRUSTED_CONTEXTS'})` on startup. (2) Detection latency — sync only ran on the 1-min alarm; added SYNC_NOW rpc fired from content on load + tab focus/visibility + on leaving `/eventedit` (likely save), throttled 4s, so the banner appears in seconds and shows immediately on return. (3) Manage opened panel but not the event — now sets ACTIVE_EID via storage.session FIRST (content), then OPEN_FOR_EVENT (no await, gesture-prompt) + NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT. Added GET_PANEL_STATE rpc so a freshly-loaded content script (after same-tab nav) knows panelOpen and auto-follow keeps working. OS notification is the cross-tab alert only (can't open panel — gesture rule).
26	
27	**In-page nudge banner (2026-06-19):** the true "one click opens both" flow. Background sync broadcasts marked events (NUDGE_TARGETS) to calendar tabs + GET_NUDGE_TARGETS rpc (listMarked, no sync) seeds it on page load. Content shows a dismissible top-center banner for the soonest non-dismissed visitor event (hidden when panel open); "Manage" → OPEN_FOR_EVENT (opens panel+sets active) + NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT (same-tab to /eventedit). Dismissals persist in storage.session (NUDGE_DISMISS_KEY). WHY in-page not OS-notification: sidePanel.open() needs a user gesture; OS-notification click can't reliably open the panel (in-page click can — proven by the Manage button). OS notification kept as the cross-tab ambient alert (fires from doSync for newMarked); its click currently only clears (gap, acceptable). Limitation: "needs passes" = magic-address marked set; doesn't yet know engine sent/materialized state, so dismissal-based suppression within a browser session.
28	
29	**Auto-follow (2026-06-19, accepted recommendation):** when the panel is open and the user opens/views a SAVED event in Calendar, the panel auto-loads that event's visitor details (the same /addon/draft config screen, which already shows sent/cancelled status — no separate "details" view). Content script: panel-open → hide button + `FOLLOW_EVENT` (saved events only, non-empty eid; deduped; reset on panel close). Background FOLLOW_EVENT sets ACTIVE_EID/snapshot without opening panel. Guard 1: panel freezes displayed eid while busy (send/preview pending or preview sheet open), catches up when idle (useState `eid` synced from `storedEid` via effect gated on !busy). Guard 2: edits persist on blur already. "Following the event open in Calendar" caption on the config screen. Unsaved events still don't auto-switch.
30	
31	Built 2026-06-19 (Ideas 2/3/4 from the flows doc; pre-save staging/Idea 1 ON HOLD): **Idea 2** — magic-address GATE on the in-page button DROPPED (button shows on any open event surface; click=intent). Button now **injected natively** before the "Save" button on the `/eventedit` page (anchor = text match 'save'), with **graceful fallback to the floating shadow-DOM button** for popover/modal (no clean anchor). Magic address kept ONLY as background proactive-nudge signal. **Idea 3** — Back button (ArrowLeft) in panel header on all non-home screens → clears ACTIVE_EID_KEY+snapshot → home list. **Idea 4** — EmptyState list relabeled "Visitor events needing passes"; clicking an item sets active eid AND fires NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT → background `chrome.tabs.update` navigates the existing calendar tab to `/r/eventedit/<eid encoded with user email>` (preserves /u/N/ via regex on current tab URL), same tab. Modal-dismiss fix: shieldInteractions (stopPropagation+preventDefault on pointerdown/mousedown/touchstart/click) so clicking our button never closes Google's popover.
32	
33	Hybrid DOM+API layering (2026-06-19, user-directed "use both, switch per scenario"): **DOM layer** (content script) = instant/realtime/pre-save — gates the button + best-effort snapshot (guest emails via regex) + magic-address detection; **API layer** (background `events.get`) = authoritative truth (name/guests/time/location + now description + rooms=resource attendees) and the only path with an iCalUID to actually issue passes. Merge: panel paints snapshot instantly → reconciles to API → unsaved (API 404) keeps DOM guests as read-only preview + "save to issue passes". Button **renamed "Register a visitor" → "Manage Visitors"**, now **gated on the magic address being present in the open event** (content script scans open-surface text for WXT_MAGIC_ADDRESS; only visitor events get the button). Detection gated to open event SURFACE (popover via `[role=dialog]` + `data-eventid`, or `/eventedit/<eid>` URL) — our own shadow-DOM button, NOT injected into Google markup. NOTE: current Calendar popover uses display NAMES not emails, so DOM email-scrape may be partial — verify `[role=dialog]` actually wraps the popover in the live UI (if button never shows on single-click, that selector needs widening).
34	
35	ENGINE setup details: new env var `AUXILIO_EXTENSION_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` (engine `addon-auth.ts` `audiences()` now merges it with the add-on var — **that engine edit is UNCOMMITTED**). OAuth client must be **Web application** type (not Chrome-extension type), redirect `https://<ext-id>.chromiumapp.org/`, JS origins empty. Load unpacked from `.output/chrome-mv3` (⌘⇧. to see dot-folder). Current Google Calendar URLs carry **no eid** (popover stays `/r/week`; editor uses `?state=[...]`) — that's why detection had to move to `data-eventid` (decodes to `<eventId> <calendarId>`). **Still never runtime-tested in Chrome.**
36	
37	Canonical docs: `Planning-docs/2026-06-18_calendar_visitor_chrome_extension_decision.md` (strategy/approval) + `Planning-docs/2026-06-19_calendar_addon_vs_extension_capabilities.md` (add-on vs extension mechanisms, capability matrix, DOM-free analysis, benefits/limitations, 3 robust architecture options — A hybrid recommended) + `Planning-docs/2026-06-19_calendar_extension_flows_and_ideas.md` (LIVING flow/UX ideas doc — append new flow ideas here; 4 ideas analyzed 2026-06-19: pre-save staging [fragile correlation, merge into nudge], drop magic-address gate on button [our-own-button not injection; magic addr kept only for proactive nudge], back button [accepted], save→sync→nudge→open event+panel same tab via tabs.update(/eventedit/<eid>) [strongest, buildable]). Key facts: add-on syncs realtime because Google pushes the open event incl. pre-save edits via eventOpenTrigger/eventUpdateTrigger (`e.calendar.attendees`) — exclusive to add-ons; extension must infer via `data-eventid` (DOM) + Calendar API (saved state only). Two hard walls in ANY extension: auto-open (gesture rule) and pre-save-live edits (no API) — not solvable by more OAuth scope. Related: [[project_calendar_visitor_addon]], [[project_calendar_visitor_invite]], [[project_calendar_flow_consolidation]], [[project_multi_flow_invite_linking]].

Done. Everything's green and the P1/P2 pass is complete.

## Summary of this pass

**P1b — recurring over-nudging → shipped (v0.5.1)**
A recurring visitor series is now one nudge unit: one notification, badge counts distinct pending series, picker/banner show the soonest pending occurrence. Uses the API's `recurringEventId`.

**P1a — durable dismissal → deliberately reversed**
I'd recommended it, but P0a made it a regression risk. Durable suppression now correctly happens only when work is *done* (`markHandled`); the `X` stays a session snooze so genuinely-pending events resurface instead of vanishing on a stray click.

**P2a — secondary calendars → deferred (your call, Option 1)**
Left primary-only with a `KNOWN LIMITATION` comment at `listEvents` spelling out the trigger to revisit and the owner-only constraint for if/when we do.

**P2b — DOM-scrape hardening → not doing it**
Would trade the content script's no-markup-coupling principle for ~1 min of latency on a backstopped edge case.

## State
- Files touched: `lib/calendar.ts`, `lib/calendar-sync.ts`, `lib/types.ts` (and the P0 files from before).
- `tsc` clean, production build clean, version at **0.5.1**.
- Memory: added `project_extension_nudge_reliability.md` + index pointer, capturing what shipped and the two reasoned reversals.
- **Nothing committed** — working tree only, per repo rules.

> AGENT

A few things are genuinely still open. Honestly, in priority order:

**1. None of this is runtime-verified in Chrome.** Everything is `tsc` + build green, but my changes haven't actually run in a browser — and per the project memory the extension was "never runtime-tested in Chrome" even before this. The two riskiest spots to verify live:
- The **reconnect banner's gesture chain** (`pointerup` → `OPEN_PANEL` → `sidePanel.open`). It reuses the same pattern as the working "Manage" button, so it *should* carry the user activation — but `sidePanel.open()` is gesture-sensitive and I'd want to see it actually open before trusting it.
- The **auth-lapse trigger firing at all** — i.e. that a real restart/expired-session produces the `!` badge + banner rather than just going quiet. That depends on `wasConnected()` being true and the silent re-mint actually throwing.

**2. P0a suppression is local-only (real residual gap).** If passes were sent via the **add-on or another device**, this extension's nudge won't know and will keep nagging. Closing it properly needs an engine "which iCalUIDs still need passes" endpoint — an engine-contract change I deliberately didn't make. This is the main correctness limit that remains by design.

**3. `markHandled` depends on `draft.providerEventId` being set.** If the engine's send response ever returns a draft without `providerEventId`, the event silently won't get suppressed (falls back to old nag-until-aged-out behavior). Worth a quick check that the engine always populates it on the send path.

**4. Dependency outside this repo:** the engine env merge (`AUXILIO_EXTENSION_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` into the audience allow-list) is flagged in memory as **uncommitted**. Sign-in won't work against the engine until that's deployed. Not something my changes touch, but it gates the whole flow.

**Deferred by your decision (not "issues," just noted):** P2a secondary calendars (documented limitation) and P2b DOM-scrape hardening (won't-fix).

My recommendation: the next concrete step is a real Chrome load-and-test of items 1–2 before committing — I can walk you through a manual verification, or use the `/verify` skill. Want me to do that, or check #3 (the engine send path for `providerEventId`) first since it's a quick code read?

1	/**
2	 * Engine client (background only) for the office-ops-engine /addon/* routes.
3	 * Reuses the existing contract verbatim — no engine changes. Authenticated with
4	 * the host's Google id_token (Bearer). Always sends the canonical iCalUid so the
5	 * extension converges on the same calendar_event_links row as .ics/OAuth/add-on.
6	 */
7	import { ADDON_API } from './config';
8	import type {
9	  ActiveEvent,
10	  DraftPatch,
11	  DraftResponse,
12	  PreviewResponse,
13	  SendResponse,
14	  VisitDraft,
15	} from './types';
16	
17	export class EngineError extends Error {
18	  constructor(
19	    message: string,
20	    readonly status: number,
21	  ) {
22	    super(message);
23	    this.name = 'EngineError';
24	  }
25	}
26	
27	async function call<T>(
28	  path: string,
29	  method: string,
30	  idToken: string,
31	  body?: unknown,
32	): Promise<T> {
33	  const url = `${ADDON_API}/${path}`;
34	  const started = Date.now();
35	  // Abort hung requests so the UI errors instead of spinning forever
36	  // (bifrost dev cold-starts + MV3 service-worker lifetime can stall a fetch).
37	  const ctrl = new AbortController();
38	  const timeout = setTimeout(() => ctrl.abort(), 20_000);
39	  console.log('[auxilio] engine →', method, url);
40	  let res: Response;
41	  try {
42	    res = await fetch(url, {
43	      method,
44	      headers: {
45	        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
46	        Authorization: `Bearer ${idToken}`,
47	      },
48	      body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
49	      signal: ctrl.signal,
50	    });
51	  } catch (err) {
52	    if ((err as Error).name === 'AbortError') {
53	      throw new EngineError(
54	        `Engine did not respond within 20s (${url}). Is it reachable?`,
55	        0,
56	      );
57	    }
58	    throw new EngineError(
59	      `Could not reach the engine (${(err as Error).message}). Check WXT_ENGINE_URL / host_permissions.`,
60	      0,
61	    );
62	  } finally {
63	    clearTimeout(timeout);
64	  }
65	  const text = await res.text();
66	  console.log('[auxilio] engine ←', res.status, `${Date.now() - started}ms`, text.slice(0, 200));
67	  let data: { error?: string } = {};
68	  try {
69	    data = text ? JSON.parse(text) : {};
70	  } catch {
71	    if (!res.ok) throw new EngineError(`Engine error ${res.status}: ${text.slice(0, 140)}`, res.status);
72	  }
73	  if (!res.ok) {
74	    throw new EngineError(data?.error ?? `Request failed (${res.status})`, res.status);
75	  }
76	  return data as T;
77	}

> AGENT

Both are very fixable. Here's the concrete design for each, grounded in the code I just read.

## Fix #3 — `providerEventId` dependency (small, fully in-extension)

The fix is to **stop depending on the engine echoing it back at all**. The background already holds the event id locally — it's in `ACTIVE_EID_KEY` (storage.session), which decodes to exactly the `eventId` the marked set is keyed by. So in the `SEND` handler, derive the id from the active eid and use `result.draft.providerEventId` only as a fallback:

```ts
case 'SEND':
  return withTokens(async (t) => {
    const result = await engine.send(t.idToken, msg.iCalUid, msg.start, msg.end);
    if (result.activeCount > 0) {
      // Prefer the eid we already hold locally (matches the marked-set key);
      // fall back to the engine's echoed providerEventId.
      const stored = await chrome.storage.session.get(ACTIVE_EID_KEY);
      const eid = stored[ACTIVE_EID_KEY] as string | undefined;
      const eventId = (eid && decodeEid(eid)?.eventId) || result.draft.providerEventId;
      if (eventId) {
        await markHandled(eventId);
        await refreshNudgeSurfaces();
      }
    }
    return result;
  });
```

`decodeEid` is already imported. This removes the silent-no-suppression failure mode entirely — the only way it doesn't fire now is if *both* the active eid and the engine's providerEventId are missing, which can't happen on a normal send. I can do this one immediately.

## Fix #2 — cross-channel suppression (needs a small engine endpoint)

The engine already knows the truth: `getDraft(iCalUid)` returns `materialized` + a roster with per-guest `status`. The clean fix is a **read-only batch status endpoint** the sync polls, kept separate from the local handled-overlay so it stays live.

**Engine side** (`routes.ts`, alongside the other `/addon/*` routes — same audience auth):
```ts
// POST /addon/status — read-only: which of these events already have active passes.
// No draft creation, no link recording (unlike /draft) → safe to poll cheaply.
if (pathname === '/api/visitor/calendar/addon/status' && method === 'POST') {
  const body = await readBodyJson(req);
  const iCalUids = (Array.isArray(body.iCalUids) ? body.iCalUids : [])
    .map(String).slice(0, 100); // cap
  const active: string[] = [];
  for (const uid of iCalUids) {
    const draft = await getDraft(uid);
    if (draft?.roster.some((g) => g.status === 'sent')) active.push(uid);
  }
  json(res, 200, { active });
  return true;
}
```
This is purely additive (new route, no signature change to existing ones), so it respects the extension's "engine owns the contract" rule — but it's an engine-repo change that has to be deployed and the route is the new dependency.

**Extension side:**
- `lib/engine.ts`: add `status: (idToken, iCalUids) => call<{active: string[]}>('status', 'POST', idToken, { iCalUids })`.
- `lib/calendar-sync.ts`: add an `engineHandled` set in storage.local that's **overwritten each poll** (not durable like `handled`) — `setEngineHandled(iCalUids)`. `listMarked()` and the badge count filter out events whose `iCalUid ∈ engineHandled` **in addition to** the local `handled` overlay.
- `background.ts` `doSync`: after `runSync`, collect the marked set's iCalUids and call `engine.status`, then `setEngineHandled(result.active)`.

The reason `engineHandled` is refreshed-each-poll rather than baked into the durable overlay: if someone **cancels** passes via the add-on, the next poll drops that iCalUid from `active` and the event correctly **resurfaces** — staleness can't strand it.

**Cost (the Ameya lens):** one extra batched POST per sync cycle, and only when there are marked events. To keep it cheap I'd gate it — call `/status` only when the marked iCalUid list **changed** since the last status check, or every ~5th tick (mirrors the split-cadence we already use for field-app sync). So in steady state it's near-zero extra calls.

---

**Recommendation:** do #3 now (it's a few lines, no deploy, removes a real silent-failure path). Do #2 as a small coordinated change when you're next touching the engine — it's the one that makes the nudge *correct* across the add-on and other devices, which matters since the add-on is the mobile surface people will actually use.

Want me to implement #3 now, and scaffold #2 across both repos (engine route + extension wiring) so it's ready to deploy together?

293	      });

297	      );
298	
299	    case 'LIST_VISITOR_EVENTS':
300	      return withTokens(async (t) => {
301	        // A refresh hiccup (a transient Calendar API error) must not blank the
302	        // list — serve the last-known marked set instead of failing the query.
303	        try {
304	          await runSync(t.accessToken); // refresh before listing
305	        } catch (err) {
306	          console.warn('[auxilio] LIST_VISITOR_EVENTS refresh failed; serving cached set', err);
307	        }
308	        return listMarked();
309	      });

```
/**
 * Calendar sync (background only). The durable, official "what changed" engine:
 * runs on a chrome.alarm, lists changes via a sync token, and maintains the set
 * of upcoming visitor (magic-address) events in storage.local. Drives:
 *  - post-save refresh of the open event (changedIds),
 *  - the visitor-event notification + toolbar badge (newMarked / count),
 *  - the side panel's "open from list" picker (listMarked).
 * No DOM. Reflects SAVED state only (pre-save edits are add-on-only — see
 * Planning-docs/2026-06-19_calendar_addon_vs_extension_capabilities.md).
 */
import { MAGIC_ADDRESS } from './config';
import { encodeEid, isMarked, listEvents } from './calendar';
import type { VisitorEventSummary } from './types';

const SYNC_TOKEN_KEY = 'auxilio.syncToken';
const MARKED_KEY = 'auxilio.markedEvents';
/** Events the user has already issued passes for from this extension — keyed by
 *  eventId, value = when handled. A durable overlay over the marked set so a
 *  finished event stops badging/notifying/nudging instead of nagging until it
 *  ages out. Local-only signal: passes sent via the add-on or another device
 *  won't appear here (full cross-channel truth needs an engine status endpoint —
 *  deferred). Pruned by HANDLED_TTL_MS. */
const HANDLED_KEY = 'auxilio.handledEvents';
/** When the last FULL (tokenless) re-scan ran. Incremental sync only reports
 *  changes, so an event missed when its change was first consumed (e.g. a
 *  different magic address in an earlier build) is never re-examined until a full
 *  re-scan. We force one periodically + on install so the set self-heals. */
const LAST_FULL_KEY = 'auxilio.lastFullSync';
const PAST_GRACE_MS = 60 * 60_000; // keep events until 1h after they end
const HANDLED_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60_000; // forget "handled" after 30 days
const FULL_RESYNC_INTERVAL_MS = 12 * 60 * 60_000; // re-scan the whole window twice a day

type MarkedMap = Record<string, VisitorEventSummary>;
type HandledMap = Record<string, number>;

async function readMarked(): Promise<MarkedMap> {
  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(MARKED_KEY);
  return (r[MARKED_KEY] as MarkedMap) ?? {};
}

async function readHandled(): Promise<HandledMap> {
  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(HANDLED_KEY);
  return (r[HANDLED_KEY] as HandledMap) ?? {};
}

/** Drop the sync token so the next sync does a full tokenless re-scan of the
 *  forward window. Called on install/update so a config change (e.g. the magic
 *  address) takes effect immediately instead of waiting for the periodic resync. */
export async function clearSyncToken(): Promise<void> {
  await chrome.storage.local.remove([SYNC_TOKEN_KEY, LAST_FULL_KEY]);
}

/** Record that passes were issued for this event — drops it from every nudge
 *  surface (badge, banner, notification, picker) until it ages out. */
export async function markHandled(eventId: string): Promise<void> {
  if (!eventId) return;
  const handled = await readHandled();
  handled[eventId] = Date.now();
  await chrome.storage.local.set({ [HANDLED_KEY]: handled });
}

export interface SyncResult {
  changedIds: Set<string>;
  newMarked: VisitorEventSummary[];
  /** Events seen this cycle that are no longer pending visitor events (deleted/
   *  cancelled). Lets the in-page nudge purge stale optimistic guesses. */
  cancelledIds: string[];
  markedCount: number;
}

/** Coalesce concurrent syncs (the 1-minute alarm + on-demand LIST/SYNC_NOW) so
 *  they can't race on the shared sync token / marked map and lose an update — both
 *  read the same baseline, both write at the end, and the later write clobbers the
 *  earlier one (an event one run marked can vanish, and won't be re-reported once
 *  the token advances). One in-flight sync at a time removes that hazard. */
```

```
 *  the token advances). One in-flight sync at a time removes that hazard. */
let inFlight: Promise<SyncResult> | null = null;
export function runSync(accessToken: string): Promise<SyncResult> {
  if (inFlight) return inFlight;
  inFlight = doRunSync(accessToken).finally(() => {
    inFlight = null;
  });
  return inFlight;
}

/** Pull changes since the last sync; update the marked set; report deltas. */
async function doRunSync(accessToken: string): Promise<SyncResult> {
  const store = await chrome.storage.local.get([
    SYNC_TOKEN_KEY,
    MARKED_KEY,
    HANDLED_KEY,
    LAST_FULL_KEY,
  ]);
  // The set we knew BEFORE this sync — the baseline for "what's genuinely new"
  // (so a full re-scan that re-discovers the whole window doesn't re-notify).
  const prevMarked = (store[MARKED_KEY] as MarkedMap) ?? {};
  const handled = (store[HANDLED_KEY] as HandledMap) ?? {};
  const lastFull = (store[LAST_FULL_KEY] as number) ?? 0;

  let syncToken = store[SYNC_TOKEN_KEY] as string | undefined;
  // Force a periodic full re-scan: incremental sync never re-reports an event
  // whose change was already consumed, so a once-missed event (wrong magic
  // address in a past build, a transient attendee trim) would stay invisible
  // forever. Dropping the token re-evaluates the whole forward window.
  if (syncToken && Date.now() - lastFull > FULL_RESYNC_INTERVAL_MS) {
    syncToken = undefined;
  }

  let resp = await listEvents(accessToken, { syncToken });
  if (resp.expired) {
    // Token stale → full resync from scratch.
    syncToken = undefined;
    resp = await listEvents(accessToken, {});
  }
  // A tokenless call re-scanned the full window; rebuild the set from scratch so
  // stale/no-longer-marked events drop out. Incremental carries the set forward.
  const fullSync = !syncToken;
  const marked: MarkedMap = fullSync ? {} : { ...prevMarked };

  const changedIds = new Set<string>();
  const newMarked: VisitorEventSummary[] = [];
  const cancelledIds: string[] = [];

  // Series we already tracked (any previously-marked instance) — so a newly
  // discovered later occurrence of a known recurring event, OR a full re-scan
  // that re-finds the whole window, doesn't fire a fresh notification.
  const knownSeries = new Set(
    Object.values(prevMarked).map((m) => m.seriesId ?? m.eventId),
  );
  // Series notified within THIS cycle — collapses an initial sync that discovers
  // dozens of weekly instances at once down to a single notification.
  const notifiedSeries = new Set<string>();

  for (const ev of resp.items) {
    if (!ev.id) continue;
    changedIds.add(ev.id);
    const cancelled = ev.status === 'cancelled';
    const marked_ = isMarked(ev);
    // DIAGNOSTIC: the magic address is somewhere in this event's payload, yet
    // isMarked() didn't flag it — i.e. it's not a structured attendee email and
    // not in `location` (e.g. it's only in the description, or events.list
    // returned a trimmed attendee list). This is the signature of a primary-
    // calendar miss; the warning shows exactly what we got back.
    if (!marked_ && MAGIC_ADDRESS && JSON.stringify(ev).toLowerCase().includes(MAGIC_ADDRESS)) {
      console.warn(
        '[auxilio] sync MISS: magic address in payload but isMarked() is false',
        { id: ev.id, summary: ev.summary, location: ev.location, attendees: ev.attendees, status: ev.status },
      );
    }
    if (marked_ && !cancelled) {
      const seriesId = ev.recurringEventId || ev.id;
      const summary: VisitorEventSummary = {
        eid: encodeEid(ev.id, 'primary'),
        eventId: ev.id,
        iCalUid: ev.iCalUID ?? '',
        title: ev.summary || '(no title)',
        start: ev.start?.dateTime ?? ev.start?.date,
        seriesId,
      };
      // Notify only for a genuinely new, not-yet-handled occurrence of a series we
      // weren't already tracking — one alert per recurring series, not per
      // instance, and never for an event a full re-scan merely re-discovered.
      const newInstance = !prevMarked[ev.id] && !handled[ev.id];
      if (newInstance && !knownSeries.has(seriesId) && !notifiedSeries.has(seriesId)) {
        newMarked.push(summary);
        notifiedSeries.add(seriesId);
      }
      marked[ev.id] = summary;
    } else {
      // Cancelled (deleted) or no longer a visitor event → not pending. Report
      // it so any optimistic in-page nudge for it gets purged, and forget any
      // "handled" record so the id can't linger.
      if (cancelled || prevMarked[ev.id]) cancelledIds.push(ev.id);
      delete marked[ev.id];
      delete handled[ev.id];
    }
  }

  // Prune events that are well past.
  const cutoff = Date.now() - PAST_GRACE_MS;
  for (const id of Object.keys(marked)) {
    const s = marked[id].start;
    if (s && Date.parse(s) < cutoff) delete marked[id];
  }

  // Prune stale "handled" records so the overlay can't grow without bound.
  const handledCutoff = Date.now() - HANDLED_TTL_MS;
  for (const id of Object.keys(handled)) {
    if (handled[id] < handledCutoff) delete handled[id];
  }

  // DIAGNOSTIC: one line per sync so a primary-calendar miss is obvious — did the
  // list even return the event (itemsReturned), and did anything end up marked?
  console.log('[auxilio] sync done', {
    mode: fullSync ? 'full' : 'incremental',
    magicAddress: MAGIC_ADDRESS,
    itemsReturned: resp.items.length,
    markedTotal: Object.keys(marked).length,
    newMarked: newMarked.length,
  });

  await chrome.storage.local.set({
    [SYNC_TOKEN_KEY]: resp.nextSyncToken ?? syncToken,
    [MARKED_KEY]: marked,
    [HANDLED_KEY]: handled,
    // Stamp the full-resync clock only when we actually did one, so the periodic
    // re-scan fires on schedule regardless of how often incremental sync runs.
    ...(fullSync ? { [LAST_FULL_KEY]: Date.now() } : {}),
  });

  // markedCount drives the toolbar badge — count distinct pending series (a
  // recurring event counts once, not once per occurrence).
  const pendingSeries = new Set<string>();
  for (const id of Object.keys(marked)) {
    if (!handled[id]) pendingSeries.add(marked[id].seriesId ?? id);
  }
  return { changedIds, newMarked, cancelledIds, markedCount: pendingSeries.size };
}

/** Upcoming visitor events still needing passes, soonest first — for the panel's
 *  picker and the in-page nudge. Handled events are filtered out, and a recurring
 *  series collapses to its soonest pending occurrence (one row, not one per week). */
export async function listMarked(): Promise<VisitorEventSummary[]> {
  const [marked, handled] = await Promise.all([readMarked(), readHandled()]);
  const bySeries = new Map<string, VisitorEventSummary>();
  for (const m of Object.values(marked)) {
    if (handled[m.eventId]) continue;
    const key = m.seriesId ?? m.eventId;
    const existing = bySeries.get(key);
    if (!existing || (m.start ?? '~').localeCompare(existing.start ?? '~') < 0) {
      bySeries.set(key, m);
    }
  }
  return [...bySeries.values()].sort((a, b) =>
    (a.start ?? '').localeCompare(b.start ?? ''),
  );
}

/** Is this event id currently a known visitor event? */
export async function isEventMarked(eventId: string): Promise<boolean> {
  const marked = await readMarked();
  return !!marked[eventId];
}

[]);\n}\n\n/** Replace the engine-handled iCalUid set with the latest status answer. Overwrite\n *  (not merge) so events whose passes were cancelled elsewhere drop out and\n *  resurface. []);\n}\n\n/** iCalUids of marked events that aren't LOCALLY handled — the candidate set to\n *  ask the engine about. Throttled: re-polls when the pending set\n *  changes, else at most every STATUS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS. Degrades gracefully — if\n *  the engine/route is unreachable it keeps the last known set (local-only\n *  suppression still works). Returns the (lower-cased) engine-handled set. */\nasync function maybePollEngineStatus(idToken: string): Promise<Set<string>> {\n  const uids = await listPendingICalUids();\n  if (uids.length === 0) {\n    await setEngineHandled([]); // nothing pending → clear the cache\n    return new Set();\n  }\n  const sig = [...uids].sort().join('|');\n  const store = await chrome.storage.local.get(STATUS_POLL_KEY);\n  const last = store[STATUS_POLL_KEY] as { at: number; sig: string } | undefined;\n  const fresh = last && Date.now() - last.at < STATUS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS;\n  if (last && last.sig === sig && fresh) return readEngineHandled(); // reuse cache\n\n  // Stamp the throttle now (before the call) so a missing/erroring route backs off\n  // instead of retrying every tick. A changed signature still forces a re-poll.\n  await chrome.storage.local.set({ [STATUS_POLL_KEY]: { at: Date.now(), sig } });\n  try {\n    const { active } = await engine.status(idToken, uids);\n    await setEngineHandled(active);\n    return new Set(active.map((u) => u.toLowerCase()));\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.warn('[auxilio] engine status poll failed; keeping last known', err);\n    return readEngineHandled();\n  }\n}"
}

> AGENT

Both fixes are implemented, type-checked, and built.

## Fix #3 — `providerEventId` dependency (extension-only, done)
`SEND` now derives the event id from the **local active eid** (`ACTIVE_EID_KEY` → `decodeEid`), which is exactly the marked-set key, and only falls back to the engine's echoed `providerEventId`. The silent "never suppressed" path is gone.

## Fix #2 — cross-channel suppression (engine + extension, done)
**Engine** (`routes.ts`): added `POST /api/visitor/calendar/addon/status` — read-only, same addon auth, body `{iCalUids[]}` (capped 100) → `{active[]}` (uids with a `sent` guest). Side-effect-free (`getDraft` only, no draft creation or link recording), so it's cheap to poll. Additive route, no existing signature touched.

**Extension:**
- `engine.status()` client method.
- `background.maybePollEngineStatus()` polls the pending iCalUids, **throttled** (re-poll on set-change, else every 5 min; throttle stamped in `storage.local` so it survives service-worker restarts; degrades to last-known set if the route errors).
- `calendar-sync` keeps an `engineHandled` set that's **overwritten every poll** (so a pass cancelled via the add-on resurfaces the nudge), and filters it in `listMarked` + the badge count. `listPendingICalUids` (the query candidates) deliberately ignores `engineHandled` so cancellations get re-detected.
- Notifications now skip any event the engine already handled.

## Verification
- Extension: `tsc` clean, build clean, **v0.7.0**.
- Engine: my added block introduces **no** new tsc errors (verified — all `routes.ts` errors are pre-existing, at unrelated lines; repo has a baseline of ~20). It builds via the bundler regardless.

## Two things gating #2 going live (both outside this code)
1. **Engine route is uncommitted and must be deployed** — until then the status poll 404s and the extension cleanly falls back to local-only suppression (no breakage).
2. **The extension's OAuth client id must be in the engine's audience allow-list** (`AUXILIO_EXTENSION_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`) — already flagged as uncommitted in the extension memory.

Memory updated to mark the cross-channel gap closed (was "deferred").

Nothing committed — both repos are working-tree only. Want me to deploy the engine (with `--commit`, per the rules) and walk through a live test, or leave it for you?
